——先生进了众人聚着的屋子，举手请大家安静，「惠灵顿的民众，方才险些对一个黑人施行报复，那黑人被认定犯了一桩滔天大罪。本城的白人，出于至高至圣的情感，正要奋起捍卫自己的家园，维护本种族的纯洁与优势。诸位此举，发自最柔嫩处受了伤的心。」
「好哇，好哇！」一个挤在人堆里的醉水手喊。
「然而也正是这同一种正义感，」卡特里特慷慨陈词，「它驱你们严惩罪人、雷厉风行，也断不容你们杀害无辜。哪怕是黑人，只要安分守己、不越本位，也该受法律的保护。惩罚罪恶，我们不妨严厉峻刻，无愧于我们这个主宰的种族；但持法的天平，我们端得四平八稳，不偏不倚。」
「好哇，不偏不倚！」醉水手又喊，当场叫人不客气地架了出去。
「我们业已查明，确凿无疑：在押的黑人桑迪·坎贝尔并未犯这桩劫杀；此案系一不知名的男子所为，现已逃出本城。我们敬爱的同胞、德高望重的德拉梅尔先生——坎贝尔在他家佣工多年——愿为坎贝尔的品行作保；并进一步声明，昨晚案发可能的整段时辰里，坎贝尔始终在他身边。」
「德拉梅尔先生若肯为此宣誓，」人群里有人说，「这黑人就不该私刑。」
嗡嗡起了不满之声。一切筹备都已就绪。私刑若不举行，大家要大失所望的。
「德拉梅尔先生要救那黑鬼，就叫他去宣嘛。」人群里又有人喊。
「当然，」卡特里特欣然同意，「这个誓德拉梅尔先生没有任何理由不宣。在场有公证人或治安法官么？」
一个人站出来。「我是治安法官。」
「好极了，史密斯先生，」卡特里特认出他来，「承您俯允，由我拟誓词，德拉梅尔先生若肯，跟我复述一遍便是。我郑重宣誓——」
「我郑重宣誓——」
德拉梅尔——

===Original===

Delamere’s voice might have come from the tomb, so hollow and unnatural did it sound.
“So help me God,”﻿—
“So help me God,”﻿—
“That the negro Sandy Campbell, now in jail on the charge of murder, robbery, and assault, was in my presence last night between the hours of eight and two o’clock.”
Mr. Delamere repeated this statement in a firm voice; but to Ellis, who was in the secret, his words fell upon the ear like clods dropping upon the coffin in an open grave.
“I wish to add,” said General Belmont, stepping forward, “that it is not our intention to interfere, by anything which may be done at this meeting, with the orderly process of the law, or to advise the prisoner’s immediate release. The prisoner will remain in custody, Mr. Delamere, Major Carteret, and I guaranteeing that he will be proved entirely innocent at the preliminary hearing tomorrow morning.”
Several of those present looked relieved; others were plainly, disappointed; but when the meeting ended, the news went out that the lynching had been given up. Carteret immediately wrote and had struck off a handbill giving a brief statement of the proceedings, and sent out a dozen boys to distribute copies among the people in the streets. That no precaution might be omitted, a call was issued to the Wellington Grays, the crack independent military company of the city, who in an incredibly short time were on guard at the jail. Thus a slight change in the point of view had demonstrated the entire ability of the leading citizens to maintain the dignified and orderly processes of the law whenever they saw fit to do so.
---
The night passed without disorder, beyond the somewhat rough handling of two or three careless negroes that came in the way of small parties of the disappointed who had sought alcoholic consolation.
At ten o’clock the next morning, a preliminary hearing of the charge against Campbell was had before a magistrate. Mr. Delamere, perceptibly older and more wizened than he had seemed the day before, and leaning heavily on the arm of a servant, repeated his statement of the evening before. Only one or two witnesses were called, among whom was Mr. Ellis, who swore positively that in his opinion the prisoner was not the man whom he had seen and at first supposed to be Campbell. The most sensational piece of testimony was that of Dr.

===Chinese===

——先生的声音像是从坟墓里出来的，空洞得怕人。
「愿上帝助我——」
「愿上帝助我——」
「被控凶杀、劫财、强暴、现押于狱中的黑人桑迪·坎贝尔，昨夜八点至两点之间，始终在我眼前。」
德拉梅尔先生用坚定的嗓音复述了这段话；可是知情如埃利斯听来，那字字句句落在耳中，像一块块土坷垃砸在敞开的墓穴里、棺材盖上。
「我愿补一句，」贝尔蒙特将军上前说，「本次会议的任何决定，都无意妨碍法律的正当程序，也不是建议即刻开释囚犯。囚犯仍予收押；德拉梅尔先生、卡特里特少校与我三人担保：明晨预审，他必被证明全然无罪。」
在场的几位露出宽慰之色；另有几位分明失望；可会一散，消息便传了出去：私刑作罢。卡特里特当即写了传单付印，简述会议经过，派出十来个孩子沿街散发。为万全起见，又调来了惠灵顿灰衣队——本城赫赫有名的独立民兵连——不多时便在监狱布了岗。可见只要头面市民们高兴，他们完全有本事维持法律庄重而有序的进程——稍稍换个角度，这一点便演示得明明白白。
---
一夜过去，没有出乱子；只是两三个不当心的黑人，撞在几伙借酒浇愁的失意人手里，吃了些亏。
第二天上午十点，治安法官预审了对坎贝尔的指控。德拉梅尔先生瞧着比前一日又苍老干缩了几分，重重拄着一名仆人的手臂，把前晚的话重述了一遍。只传了一两名证人，其中有埃利斯先生；他斩钉截铁地宣誓说，依他之见，囚犯不是他看见、起初当作坎贝尔的那个人。最叫人耸动的证词出自普赖斯——

===Original===

Price, who had examined the body, and who swore that the wound in the head was not necessarily fatal, and might have been due to a fall﻿—that she had more than likely died of shock attendant upon the robbery, she being of advanced age and feeble health. There was no evidence, he said, of any other personal violence.
Sandy was not even bound over to the grand jury, but was discharged upon the ground that there was not sufficient evidence upon which to hold him. Upon his release he received the congratulations of many present, some of whom would cheerfully have done him to death a few hours before. With the childish fickleness of a mob, they now experienced a satisfaction almost as great as, though less exciting than, that attendant upon taking life. We speak of the mysteries of inanimate nature. The workings of the human heart are the profoundest mystery of the universe. One moment they make us despair of our kind, and the next we see in them the reflection of the divine image. Sandy, having thus escaped from the Mr. Hyde of the mob, now received the benediction of its Dr. Jekyll. Being no cynical philosopher, and realizing how nearly the jaws of death had closed upon him, he was profoundly grateful for his escape, and felt not the slightest desire to investigate or criticise any man’s motives.
With the testimony of Dr. Price, the worst feature of the affair came to an end. The murder eliminated or rendered doubtful, the crime became a mere vulgar robbery, the extent of which no one could estimate, since no living soul knew how much money Mrs. Ochiltree had had in the cedar chest. The absurdity of the remaining charge became more fully apparent in the light of the reaction from the excitement of the day before.
Nothing further was ever done about the case; but though the crime went unpunished, it carried evil in its train. As we have seen, the charge against Campbell had been made against the whole colored race. All over the United States the Associated Press had flashed the report of another dastardly outrage by a burly black brute﻿—all black brutes it seems are burly﻿—and of the impending lynching with its prospective horrors. This news, being highly sensational in its character, had been displayed in large black type on the front pages of the daily papers.

===Chinese===

——医生；他验过尸，宣誓说，头上的伤未必致命，也可能是跌的——她极可能是死于劫案引起的惊吓，她年事已高，身子又弱。他说，没有任何其他暴力的痕迹。
桑迪连送大陪审团都不必，以证据不足为由，当庭开释。释放时，在场的许多人向他道贺；这些人里，有几个几个钟头前还巴不得把他弄死。暴民的孩子脾气，说变就变；此刻他们体味到一种满足，虽不及杀人来得刺激，却几乎同样大。我们常说无生命自然的奥秘；人心的运转，才是宇宙间最深的奥秘。这一刻它叫我们对人类绝望，下一刻我们又在其中照见神的形象。桑迪既从暴民的海德先生爪下逃出性命，如今又领受它杰基尔博士的祝福。他不是玩世的哲人，又深知死亡的牙关曾在离他多近的地方合拢；他对脱险感激涕零，半点不想追究或褒贬任何人的动机。
随着普赖斯医生的证词，这桩事最恶劣的一页翻过去了。凶杀既被排除或成疑案，罪案便只剩一桩庸俗的劫夺，数目多大谁也说不上——杉木箱里到底有过多少钱，没有一个活人知道。头一天的亢奋冷下来，剩下那条指控的荒唐，便愈发显豁。
这案子后来再无下文；可是罪虽未惩，恶果却跟在后头。如前所见，加在坎贝尔头上的指控，是加给整个有色种族的。合众国各地，通讯社的电波飞传着一个壮硕黑畜生又干下一桩卑鄙兽行的消息——黑畜生仿佛个个壮硕——以及眼看要临头的那场私刑和它的种种可怖。这消息因其骇人听闻，各日报都用大号黑字登在头版。

===Original===

The dispatch that followed, to the effect that the accused had been found innocent and the lynching frustrated, received slight attention, if any, in a fine-print paragraph on an inside page. The facts of the case never came out at all. The family honor of the Delameres was preserved, and the prestige of the white race in Wellington was not seriously impaired.
---
Upon leaving the preliminary hearing, old Mr. Delamere had requested General Belmont to call at his house during the day upon professional business. This the general did in the course of the afternoon.
“Belmont,” said Mr. Delamere, “I wish to make my will. I should have drawn it with my own hand; but you know my motives, and can testify to my soundness of mind and memory.”
He thereupon dictated a will, by the terms of which he left to his servant, Sandy Campbell, three thousand dollars, as a mark of the testator’s appreciation of services rendered and sufferings endured by Sandy on behalf of his master. After some minor dispositions, the whole remainder of the estate was devised to Dr. William Miller, in trust for the uses of his hospital and training-school for nurses, on condition that the institution be incorporated and placed under the management of competent trustees. Tom Delamere was not mentioned in the will.
“There, Belmont,” he said, “that load is off my mind. Now, if you will call in some witnesses﻿—most of my people can write﻿—I shall feel entirely at ease.”
The will was signed by Mr. Delamere, and witnessed by Jeff and Billy, two servants in the house, neither of whom received any information as to its contents, beyond the statement that they were witnessing their master’s will. “I wish to leave that with you for safe keeping, Belmont,” said Mr. Delamere, after the witnesses had retired. “Lock it up in your safe until I die, which will not be very long, since I have no further desire to live.”
An hour later Mr. Delamere suffered a third paralytic stroke, from which he died two days afterwards, without having in the meantime recovered the power of speech.
The will was never produced. The servants stated, and General Belmont admitted, that Mr. Delamere had made a will a few days before his death; but since it was not discoverable, it seemed probable that the testator had destroyed it.

===Chinese===

随后的一电，说被告已证清白、私刑未遂，只在里页一个末栏小字里混了个面熟，或者干脆无人理会。案情真相始终没有披露。德拉梅尔家的门风保住了，白人种族在惠灵顿的威望，也没有大损。
---
预审散后，老德拉梅尔先生请贝尔蒙特将军白天到他家去一趟，有正事相商。将军于当日下午到了。
「贝尔蒙特，」德拉梅尔先生说，「我要立遗嘱。本该我亲手起草；只是我的用心你知道，我的心神记忆是否清明，你也可以作证。」
他随即口授了一份遗嘱：遗赠他的仆人桑迪·坎贝尔三千元，以志立遗嘱人对他为主人效力受苦的谢忱。几笔零星处分之后，产业全部余数赠与威廉·米勒医生，以信托方式用于他的医院和护士训练学校，条件是医院须注册为法人、交由胜任的受托人管理。遗嘱里没有提汤姆·德拉梅尔。
「好了，贝尔蒙特，」他说，「这块石头落了地。请你唤几位证人进来——我家里的人多半会写字——我就完全安心了。」
遗嘱由德拉梅尔先生签押，由家里两名仆人杰夫和比利作证；两人只被告知见证的是主人的遗嘱，内容一概不知。「这份遗嘱就存在你那里，贝尔蒙特，」证人退下后，德拉梅尔先生说，「锁进你的保险柜，等我死——那不会久了，我已没有再活下去的想头。」
一个钟头后，德拉梅尔先生第三次中风，两日后去世，始终没有恢复说话的能力。
遗嘱始终没有面世。仆人们说，贝尔蒙特将军也承认，德拉梅尔先生死前几天的确立过一份遗嘱；只是遍寻无着，多半是立遗嘱人自己毁了。

===Original===

This was all the more likely, the general was inclined to think, because the will had been of a most unusual character. What the contents of the will were, he of course did not state, it having been made under the seal of professional secrecy.
This suppression was justified by the usual race argument: Miller’s hospital was already well established, and, like most negro institutions, could no doubt rely upon Northern philanthropy for any further support it might need. Mr. Delamere’s property belonged of right to the white race, and by the higher law should remain in the possession of white people. Loyalty to one’s race was a more sacred principle than deference to a weak old man’s whims.
Having reached this conclusion, General Belmont’s first impulse was to destroy the will; on second thoughts he locked it carefully away in his safe. He would hold it awhile. It might some time be advisable to talk the matter over with young Delamere, who was of a fickle disposition and might wish to change his legal adviser.

===Chinese===

将军心想，这份遗嘱性质那样特别，自己毁掉的可能就更大。遗嘱内容他当然不说——那是职业秘密的封印之下立的。
这桩隐匿，照例的种族道理一讲就通：米勒的医院根基已固，同多数黑人事业一样，往后缺什么，自有北方的慈善可依。德拉梅尔先生的产业，按权利属于白人种族；按更高的法则，理应留在白人手里。忠于种族，是比迁就一个衰朽老人的胡思乱想更神圣的原则。
将军得出了这个结论，第一个冲动便是毁掉遗嘱；转念一想，又把它仔细锁进了保险柜。先押一押。日后同小德拉梅尔谈谈这件事，兴许是得策的——那年轻人性情没准，说不定正想换一位法律顾问呢。

## XXXIV The Valley of the Shadow / 死荫的幽谷
===Original===

Miller knocked at the door. There was no response. He went round to the rear of the house. The dog had slunk behind the woodpile. Miller knocked again, at the back door, and, receiving no reply, called aloud.
“Mrs. Butler! It is I, Dr. Miller. Is my wife here?”
The slats of a nearby blind opened cautiously.
“Is it really you, Dr. Miller?”
“Yes, Mrs. Butler. I am looking for my wife and child﻿—are they here?”
“No, sir; she became alarmed about you, soon after the shooting commenced, and I could not keep her. She left for home half an hour ago. It is coming on dusk, and she and the child are so near white that she did not expect to be molested.”
“Which way did she go?”
“She meant to go by the main street. She thought it would be less dangerous than the back streets. I tried to get her to stay here, but she was frantic about you, and nothing I could say would keep her. Is the riot almost over, Dr. Miller? Do you think they will murder us all, and burn down our houses?”
“God knows,” replied Miller, with a groan. “But I must find her, if I lose my own life in the attempt.”
Surely, he thought, Janet would be safe. The white people of Wellington were not savages; or at least their temporary reversion to savagery would not go as far as to include violence to delicate women and children. Then there flashed into his mind Josh Green’s story of his “silly” mother, who for twenty years had walked the earth as a child, as the result of one night’s terror, and his heart sank within him.
Miller realized that his buggy, by attracting attention, had been a hindrance rather than a help in his progress across the city. In order to follow his wife, he must practically retrace his steps over the very route he had come. Night was falling. It would be easier to cross the town on foot. In the dusk his own color, slight in the daytime, would not attract attention, and by dodging in the shadows he might avoid those who might wish to intercept him. But he must reach Janet and the boy at any risk. He had not been willing to throw his life away hopelessly, but he would cheerfully have sacrificed it for those whom he loved.
He had gone but a short distance, and had not yet reached the centre of mob activity, when he intercepted a band of negro laborers from the cotton compress, with big Josh Green at their head.
“Hello, doctuh!” cried Josh, “does you wan’ ter jine us?”

===Chinese===

米勒敲了敲门。没有回应。他绕到屋后。狗溜到柴堆后面去了。米勒又敲后门，仍无人应，便扬声喊。
「巴特勒太太！是我，米勒医生。我的妻子在这里么？」
近处一道百叶窗的板条小心地开了。
「真是您么，米勒医生？」
「是我，巴特勒太太。我在找我的妻子孩子——她们在这儿么？」
「不在，先生；枪声一响，她为您担足了心，我拦不住她。半个钟头前她动身回家了。天擦黑了，她和孩子肤色都白得近乎白人，谅来不至于有人搅扰。」
「她走的哪条路？」
「她打算走大街。她想着总比后街小巷稳当些。我劝她留下，可她为您急疯了，我说什么也不中用。乱子快平了么，米勒医生？您看他们会不会把我们斩尽杀绝，把我们的房子烧光？」
「天知道，」米勒呻吟着答道，「可我必须找到她，哪怕找丢了这条命。」
他心想，珍妮特总该是平安的吧。惠灵顿的白人不是野人；或者至少，他们一时返祖的兽性，不至于施到弱质的女人孩子头上。可乔希·格林讲的那个「傻」母亲的故事忽然闪进他脑中——一夜的恐怖，害她二十年如孩童般行走人世——他的心一下子沉了下去。
米勒明白，他那辆惹眼的单马车，在他穿城的路上一向是帮手，如今倒成了累赘。要追妻子，他几乎得原路折返，重走一遍来路。夜幕正在落下。徒步穿城倒容易些。暮色里，他白天尚且浅淡的肤色更引不来注意；闪在阴影里，兴许还能躲开存心拦他的人。但无论冒什么险，他必须赶到珍妮特和孩子身边。无望地白扔性命，他不愿意；为所爱的人舍命，他甘之如饴。
他走出不远，还没到暴民活动的中心，便迎面撞上一伙棉花打包厂的黑人劳工，领头的正是大个子乔希·格林。
「喂，医生！」乔希喊道，「您要入伙么？」

===Original===

“I’m looking for my wife and child, Josh. They’re somewhere in this den of murderers. Have any of you seen them?”
No one had seen them.
“You men are running a great risk,” said Miller. “You are rushing on to certain death.”
“Well, suh, maybe we is; but we’re gwine ter die fightin’. Dey say de w’ite folks is gwine ter bu’n all de cullud schools an’ chu’ches, an’ kill all de niggers dey kin ketch. Dey’re gwine ter bu’n yo’ new hospittle, ef somebody don’ stop ’em.”
“Josh﻿—men﻿—you are throwing your lives away. It is a fever; it will wear off tomorrow, or tonight. They’ll not burn the schoolhouses, nor the hospital﻿—they are not such fools, for they benefit the community; and they’ll only kill the colored people who resist them. Every one of you with a gun or a pistol carries his death warrant in his own hand. I’d rather see the hospital burn than have one of you lose his life. Resistance only makes the matter worse﻿—the odds against you are too long.”
“Things can’t be any wuss, doctuh,” replied one of the crowd sturdily. “A gun is mo’ dange’ous ter de man in front of it dan ter de man behin’ it. Dey’re gwine ter kill us anyhow; an’ we’re tired﻿—we read de newspapers﻿—an’ we’re tired er bein’ shot down like dogs, widout jedge er jury. We’d ruther die fightin’ dan be stuck like pigs in a pen!”
“God help you!” said Miller. “As for me, I must find my wife and child.”
“Goodbye, doctuh,” cried Josh, brandishing a huge knife. “ ’Member ’bout de ole ’oman, ef you lives thoo dis. Don’ fergit de headbo’d an’ de footbo’d, an’ a silver plate on de coffin, ef dere’s money ernuff.”
They went their way, and Miller hurried on. They might resist attack; he thought it extremely unlikely that they would begin it; but he knew perfectly well that the mere knowledge that some of the negroes contemplated resistance would only further inflame the infuriated whites. The colored men might win a momentary victory, though it was extremely doubtful; and they would as surely reap the harvest later on. The qualities which in a white man would win the applause of the world would in a negro be taken as the marks of savagery. So thoroughly diseased was public opinion in matters of race that the negro who died for the common rights of humanity might look for no meed of admiration or glory.

===Chinese===

「我在找我的妻子孩子，乔希。她们就在这伙杀人犯的窝里什么地方。你们谁见过没有？」
谁也没有见过。
「你们几位是在冒大险，」米勒说，「这是往死路上冲。」
「唉，老爷，兴许是；可俺们死也死在打架上。人家说白人要烧掉有色人所有的学堂和教堂，见着黑人就杀。您那座新医院也要烧，没人拦就得烧。」
「乔希——弟兄们——你们这是白扔性命。这是一场热病；明天就退，至多今晚。学堂不会烧，医院也不会——他们没有那么蠢，那是给全城造福的；他们杀的，只是抵抗他们的有色人。你们哪一个，枪里枪外，手里都攢着自己的死刑判决书。我宁肯看医院烧了，也不肯看你们哪一位丢了命。抵抗只把事情弄得更糟——敌众我寡，差得太远了。」
「再糟也糟不到哪儿去了，医生，」人群里一个汉子倔强地答，「枪这东西，对着枪口的那位比端枪的这位危险。横竖是死；俺们受够了——俺们也看报——受够了像狗一样叫人崩了，不经官不问案。俺们宁可死在打架上，也不当猪圈里的猪叫人捅！」
「愿上帝护你们！」米勒说，「至于我，必须去寻我的妻子孩子。」
「再会，医生，」乔希喊道，挥着一把大刀，「您要是活过今儿个，记着老婆子的事。别忘了床头牌脚尾牌，棺材上镶银牌，钱要是凑手。」
他们去了他们的路，米勒加紧赶他的。他们或可抵御攻击；他看他们先动手是万万不会的；可他心里雪亮：单单知道有几个黑人打算抵抗，就只会给狂怒的白人再添一把火。这些有色人兴许赢一时一刻——那已是难说得很；往后的账，却是笃定要还的。白人身上能赢得满世喝彩的品质，安在黑人身上，便成了兽性的记号。舆论在种族的事情上病入骨髓：一个为人类共通权利而死的黑人，休想博得半声赞叹、半点荣名。

===Original===

At such a time, in the white man’s eyes, a negro’s courage would be mere desperation; his love of liberty, a mere animal dislike of restraint. Every finer human instinct would be interpreted in terms of savagery. Or, if forced to admire, they would none the less repress. They would applaud his courage while they stretched his neck, or carried off the fragments of his mangled body as souvenirs, in much the same way that savages preserve the scalps or eat the hearts of their enemies.
But concern for the fate of Josh and his friends occupied only a secondary place in Miller’s mind for the moment. His wife and child were somewhere ahead of him. He pushed on. He had covered about a quarter of a mile more, and far down the street could see the signs of greater animation, when he came upon the body of a woman lying upon the sidewalk. In the dusk he had almost stumbled over it, and his heart came up in his mouth. A second glance revealed that it could not be his wife. It was a fearful portent, however, of what her fate might be. The “war” had reached the women and children. Yielding to a professional instinct, he stooped, and saw that the prostrate form was that of old Aunt Jane Letlow. She was not yet quite dead, and as Miller, with a tender touch, placed her head in a more comfortable position, her lips moved with a last lingering flicker of consciousness:﻿—
“Comin’, missis, comin’!”
Mammy Jane had gone to join the old mistress upon whose memory her heart was fixed; and yet not all her reverence for her old mistress, nor all her deference to the whites, nor all their friendship for her, had been able to save her from this raging devil of race hatred which momentarily possessed the town.
Perceiving that he could do no good, Miller hastened onward, sick at heart. Whenever he saw a party of white men approaching﻿—these brave reformers never went singly﻿—he sought concealment in the shadow of a tree or the shrubbery in some yard until they had passed. He had covered about two thirds of the distance homeward, when his eyes fell upon a group beneath a lamppost, at sight of which he turned pale with horror, and rushed forward with a terrible cry.

===Chinese===

在那样的时节，在白人眼里，黑人的勇武不过是困兽之斗；他对自由的热爱，不过是牲口不爱受拘束的本能。人身上每一样较高贵的本能，都要被解说成野蛮。或者，他们纵然不得不叹服，也照旧要镇压。他们一面给他勇武喝彩，一面拉长他的脖子，或者把他残破的尸块当纪念品分走——同野人保存敌人的头皮、生吃敌人的心，正是一个路数。
不过乔希和他那伙人的命运，此刻在米勒心里只占次席。他的妻子孩子就在前面什么地方。他往前赶。又走了约四分之一英里，已望得见远处街上愈发沸腾的动静，他撞见人行道上躺着一个女人的尸身。暮色里他几乎被绊倒，心一下涌到嗓子眼。再一看，不是他的妻子。然而它是一个凶兆，凶险地预示着她可能的命运。「战争」烧到妇孾身上了。职业的本能叫他俰下身去——倒着的身影是老简·莱特洛婆婆。她还没断气；米勒轻手轻脚把她的头挪得舒坦些，她的嘴唇款动着，吐出残存的最后一丝意识：——
「来了，太太，来了！」
简奶奶找她的老主母去了；她一颗心一辈子拴在那位主母的记忆上。然而她对老主母的忠敬，她对白人的一味顺从，白人对她的种种情分，都不曾救得了她——救她不得这场一时附了这座城的、狂怒的种族仇恨的恶魔。
见自己帮不上什么，米勒怀着满心的恶心赶路。每逢一伙白人走近——这些勇敢的改革家从不单行——他便闪进树影或谁家院里的灌木丛，等他们过去。回家的路走了约三分之二，他的眼光落在灯柱底下一小群人身上；一望见，他脸色惨白，发出一声惨叫，抢步冲了过去。

## IV Theodore Felix / 西奥多·费利克斯
===Original===

The young heir of the Carterets had thriven apace, and at six months old was, according to Mammy Jane, whose experience qualified her to speak with authority, the largest, finest, smartest, and altogether most remarkable baby that had ever lived in Wellington. Mammy Jane had recently suffered from an attack of inflammatory rheumatism, as the result of which she had returned to her own home. She nevertheless came now and then to see Mrs. Carteret. A younger nurse had been procured to take her place, but it was understood that Jane would come whenever she might be needed.
“You really mean that about Dodie, do you, Mammy Jane?” asked the delighted mother, who never tired of hearing her own opinion confirmed concerning this wonderful child, which had come to her like an angel from heaven.
“Does I mean it!” exclaimed Mammy Jane, with a tone and an expression which spoke volumes of reproach. “Now, Mis’ ’Livy, what is I ever uttered er said er spoke er done dat would make you s’pose I could tell you a lie ’bout yo’ own chile?”
“No, Mammy Jane, I’m sure you wouldn’t.”
“ ’Deed, ma’am, I’m tellin’ you de Lawd’s truf. I don’ haf ter tell no lies ner strain no p’ints ’bout my ole mist’ess’s gran’chile. Dis yer boy is de ve’y spit an’ image er yo’ brother, young Mars Alick, w’at died w’en he wuz ’bout eight mont’s ole, w’iles I wuz laid off havin’ a baby er my own, an’ couldn’ be roun’ ter look after ’im. An’ dis chile is a rale quality chile, he is﻿—I never seed a baby wid sech fine hair fer his age, ner sech blue eyes, ner sech a grip, ner sech a heft. W’y, dat chile mus’ weigh ’bout twenty-fo’ poun’s, an’ he not but six mont’s ole. Does dat gal w’at does de nussin’ w’iles I’m gone ten’ ter dis chile right, Mis’ ’Livy?”
“She does fairly well, Mammy Jane, but I could hardly expect her to love the baby as you do. There’s no one like you, Mammy Jane.”
“ ’Deed dere ain’t, honey; you is talkin’ de gospel truf now! None er dese yer young folks ain’ got de trainin’ my ole mist’ess give me. Dese yer new-fangle’ schools don’ l’arn ’em nothin’ ter compare wid it. I’m jes’ gwine ter give dat gal a piece er my min’, befo’ I go, so she’ll ten’ ter dis chile right.”
The nurse came in shortly afterwards, a neat-looking brown girl, dressed in a clean calico gown, with a nurse’s cap and apron.

===Chinese===

卡特里特家的小继承人见风就长。六个月大，照简奶奶的说法——她的阅历足够她说一不二——已是惠灵顿有史以来个头最大、模样最好、心思最灵、方方面面最了不得的娃娃。简奶奶近来害了一场风湿，只好回自己家去将息，却还不时长来看看卡特里特太太。顶她差事的换了个年轻些的保姆；不过说好了，几时用得着简，几时她就来。
「您这话当真，简奶奶？」做母亲的喜滋滋地问。关于这个孩子——她像天使从天上降下来给她的——无论谁证实她自己的看法，她都听不厌。
「当真！」简奶奶嚷道，那声口那脸色，足足有一本书的委屈，「嗐，莉薇太太，俺这辈子哪句话、哪桩事，叫您疑心俺会拿您自家的娃娃哄您？」
「不会的，简奶奶，我知道您不会。」
「实在的，太太，俺说的是老天爷的真理。俺们老主母的乖孙孙，俺犯不着说瞎话，也犯不着添油加醋。这孩子活脱脱是他舅舅的模子倒出来的——小亚历克少爷，八个月上没的；那阵子俺正好歇工，生俺自个儿的孩子，没能守着他。这孩子可是地道的上等人家的孩子——这么大月龄的娃娃，俺没见过谁的头发这么细，眼睛这么蓝，手劲这么大，分量这么足。嗐，这孩子怕不有二十四磅重，才六个月呀！俺不在的时候，顶差带这孩子的那个丫头，尽心么，莉薇太太？」
「还算尽心，简奶奶；只是要她像您这样疼这孩子，到底难为她。谁也比不上您，简奶奶。」
「可比不上，宝贝儿；您这会子说的可是福音真理！如今这些年轻人，哪个有俺们老主母给俺的调教？时新学堂里学的那点子，比都没得比。俺走前非得给那丫头几句好话听听，叫她把这孩子带周全。」
不多时保姆进来了，一个齐整的棕色姑娘，穿一身干净印花布衫，戴保姆的帽子和围裙。

===Original===

“Look a-here, gal,” said Mammy Jane sternly, “I wants you ter understan’ dat you got ter take good keer er dis chile; fer I nussed his mammy dere, an’ his gran’mammy befo’ ’im, an’ you is got a priv’lege dat mos’ lackly you don’ ’preciate. I wants you to ’member, in yo’ incomin’s an’ outgoin’s, dat I got my eye on you, an’ am gwine ter see dat you does yo’ wo’k right.”
“Do you need me for anything, ma’am?” asked the young nurse, who had stood before Mrs. Carteret, giving Mammy Jane a mere passing glance, and listening impassively to her harangue. The nurse belonged to the younger generation of colored people. She had graduated from the mission school, and had received some instruction in Dr. Miller’s class for nurses. Standing, like most young people of her race, on the border line between two irreconcilable states of life, she had neither the picturesqueness of the slave, nor the unconscious dignity of those of whom freedom has been the immemorial birthright; she was in what might be called the chip-on-the-shoulder stage, through which races as well as individuals must pass in climbing the ladder of life﻿—not an interesting, at least not an agreeable stage, but an inevitable one, and for that reason entitled to a paragraph in a story of Southern life, which, with its as yet imperfect blending of old with new, of race with race, of slavery with freedom, is like no other life under the sun.
Had this old woman, who had no authority over her, been a little more polite, or a little less offensive, the nurse might have returned her a pleasant answer. These old-time negroes, she said to herself, made her sick with their slavering over the white folks, who, she supposed, favored them and made much of them because they had once belonged to them﻿—much the same reason why they fondled their cats and dogs. For her own part, they gave her nothing but her wages, and small wages at that, and she owed them nothing more than equivalent service. It was purely a matter of business; she sold her time for their money. There was no question of love between them.
Receiving a negative answer from Mrs. Carteret, she left the room without a word, ignoring Mammy Jane completely, and leaving that venerable relic of antebellum times gasping in helpless astonishment.
“Well, I nevuh!”

===Chinese===

「你听着，丫头，」简奶奶正色道，「你得明白，这孩子你得好好照看。他妈妈是俺带大的，他外婆更是俺带大的；你摊上这份差事，多半还不知宝。你给俺记着：你进进出出，俺的眼睛都盯着你；你干活的手脚，俺是要查的。」
「太太有什么吩咐么？」年轻的保姆问。她一直立在卡特里特太太跟前，对简奶奶只瞟了一眼，那番训话更是充耳不闻。这位保姆属于年轻一代的有色人。她从教会学校毕业，又在米勒医生的护士班里受过些训练。她同她那个种族多数年轻人一样，站在两种无法调和的生活状态的交界上：既没有老奴隶的画意，也没有生来自由之人那份不自觉的尊严；她正处在所谓「好勇斗狠」的阶段——个人也好，种族也好，攀人生这架梯子都免不得经过这一级——不是有趣的、至少不是讨喜的一级，却是免不了的一级；因此在这篇南方生活的故事里也配得上一段插笔。南方生活，旧与新、种族与种族、奴役与自由都还未打成一片，是天底下独一份的生活。
这老婆子，在她头上原没有半点权威，倘若客气些、少些惹人嫌的地方，保姆原本也肯回她一句好话。这些老派黑人，她心里想，叫她恶心——一味向白人摇尾乞怜；白人容他们、抬举他们，她料想，无非因为他们从前是白人的东西——同白人抚爱自家的猫儿狗儿是一个道理。至于她本人，白人给她的只有工钱，工钱还少得可怜；她欠他们的，也不过等价的活计。这是纯而又纯的买卖：她拿时间换他们的钱。中间没有半点情分。
卡特里特太太说没有吩咐，她一言不发地出了屋，把简奶奶全然不放在眼里，撂下这位战前岁月的可敬遗老，目瞪口呆，喘不上气来。
「嗐，俺活了这把年纪！」

===Original===

she ejaculated, as soon as she could get her breath, “ef dat ain’ de beatinis’ pe’fo’mance I ever seed er heared of! Dese yer young niggers ain’ got de manners dey wuz bawned wid! I don’ know w’at dey’re comin’ to, w’en dey ain’ got no mo’ rispec’ fer ole age﻿—I don’ know﻿—I don’ know!”
“Now what are you croaking about, Jane?” asked Major Carteret, who came into the room and took the child into his arms.
Mammy Jane hobbled to her feet and bobbed a curtsy. She was never lacking in respect to white people of proper quality; but Major Carteret, the quintessence of aristocracy, called out all her reserves of deference. The major was always kind and considerate to these old family retainers, brought up in the feudal atmosphere now so rapidly passing away. Mammy Jane loved Mrs. Carteret; toward the major she entertained a feeling bordering upon awe.
“Well, Jane,” returned the major sadly, when the old nurse had related her grievance, “the old times have vanished, the old ties have been ruptured. The old relations of dependence and loyal obedience on the part of the colored people, the responsibility of protection and kindness upon that of the whites, have passed away forever. The young negroes are too self-assertive. Education is spoiling them, Jane; they have been badly taught. They are not content with their station in life. Some time they will overstep the mark. The white people are patient, but there is a limit to their endurance.”
“Dat’s w’at I tells dese young niggers,” groaned Mammy Jane, with a portentous shake of her turbaned head, “w’en I hears ’em gwine on wid deir foolishniss; but dey don’ min’ me. Dey ’lows dey knows mo’ d’n I does, ’ca’se dey be’n l’arnt ter look in a book. But, pshuh! my ole mist’ess showed me mo’ d’n dem niggers’ll l’arn in a thousan’ years! I ’s fetch’ my gran’son’ Jerry up ter be ’umble, an’ keep in ’is place. An’ I tells dese other niggers dat ef dey’d do de same, an’ not crowd de w’ite folks, dey’d git ernuff ter eat, an’ live out deir days in peace an’ comfo’t. But dey don’ min’ me﻿—dey don’ min’ me!”
“If all the colored people were like you and Jerry, Jane,” rejoined the major kindly, “there would never be any trouble. You have friends upon whom, in time of need, you can rely implicitly for protection and succor.

===Chinese===

她缓过气来，失声道，「这样没规矩的事，俺见也没见过，听也没听说过！如今这些小黑人，把娘胎里带来的那点规矩都丢了！他们对老人家一点敬意也没有了，往后还不知要变成什么——俺不知道——俺不知道！」
「简，你这是咕哝什么呀？」卡特里特少校走进屋来，把孩子抱在怀里，问道。
简奶奶颤巍巍起身，屈膝道了个万福。对够格的白人，她的礼数从不欠缺；而卡特里特少校，贵族中的贵族，更能把她满腔的敬意都唤出来。少校对这些旧日家生的老仆人——在那正在飞逝的封建空气里长大的一辈——向来和气体贴。简奶奶爱卡特里特太太；对少校，她怀着的却近乎是一种敬畏。
「唉，简，」老保姆诉完委屈，少校伤感地说，「旧时代去了，旧纽带断了。有色人那边倚赖和忠顺的旧关系，白人这边保护和仁善的旧责任，永远地过去了。年轻的黑人太自作主张。教育把他们惯坏了，简；他们受的教习不对头。他们不安分于自己的身份。早晚要越过界线。白人是有耐性的，可忍耐总有个限度。」
「俺跟这些小黑人也是这么说的，」简奶奶呻吟道，裹头巾的脑袋沉沉地摇着，「听见他们胡闹，俺就说；可他们不听俺的。他们自认比俺懂得多，就因为他们念过两天书。可是呸！俺们老主母教俺的，比这些黑鬼一千年学得会还多！俺把孙子杰里也管教得服服帖帖、守他本分。俺还告诉旁的黑人，都照这样，别挤兑白人，就吃得饱、穿得暖，太太平平过一辈子。可他们不听俺的——不听俺的！」
「倘若有色人都像你和杰里这样，简，」少校和颜悦色地接道，「就永远不会有什么乱子。你们有朋友；危难临头，尽管一心一意倚靠他们护持接济。

===Original===

You served your mistress faithfully before the war; you remained by her when the other negroes were running hither and thither like sheep without a shepherd; and you have transferred your allegiance to my wife and her child. We think a great deal of you, Jane.”
“Yes, indeed, Mammy Jane,” assented Mrs. Carteret, with sincere affection, glancing with moist eyes from the child in her husband’s arms to the old nurse, whose dark face was glowing with happiness at these expressions of appreciation, “you shall never want so long as we have anything. We would share our last crust with you.”
“Thank y’, Mis’ ’Livy,” said Jane with reciprocal emotion, “I knows who my frien’s is, an’ I ain’ gwine ter let nothin’ worry me. But fer de Lawd’s sake, Mars Philip, gimme dat chile, an’ lemme pat ’im on de back, er he’ll choke hisse’f ter death!”
The old nurse had been the first to observe that little Dodie, for some reason, was gasping for breath. Catching the child from the major’s arms, she patted it on the back, and shook it gently. After a moment of this treatment, the child ceased to gasp, but still breathed heavily, with a strange, whistling noise.
“Oh, my child!” exclaimed the mother, in great alarm, taking the baby in her own arms, “what can be the matter with him, Mammy Jane?”
“Fer de Lawd’s sake, ma’am, I don’ know, ’less he’s swallered somethin’; an’ he ain’ had nothin’ in his han’s but de rattle Mis’ Polly give ’im.”
Mrs. Carteret caught up the ivory rattle, which hung suspended by a ribbon from the baby’s neck.
“He has swallowed the little piece off the end of the handle,” she cried, turning pale with fear, “and it has lodged in his throat. Telephone Dr. Price to come immediately, Philip, before my baby chokes to death! Oh, my baby, my precious baby!”
An anxious half hour passed, during which the child lay quiet, except for its labored breathing. The suspense was relieved by the arrival of Dr. Price, who examined the child carefully.
“It’s a curious accident,” he announced at the close of his inspection. “So far as I can discover, the piece of ivory has been drawn into the trachea, or windpipe, and has lodged in the mouth of the right bronchus. I’ll try to get it out without an operation, but I can’t guarantee the result.”
At the end of another half hour Dr. Price announced his inability to remove the obstruction without resorting to more serious measures.

===Chinese===

「打仗前你忠心侍奉你的主母；别的黑人像没牧人的羊东奔西窜的时候，只有你守在她身边；如今你又把这忠心移给了俺太太和她的孩子。我们很看重你，简。」
「是啊，真是的，简奶奶，」卡特里特太太真心真意地附和，含着泪眼，从丈夫怀里的孩子望到老保姆——老保姆的黑脸正被这几句体贴照得亮堂堂的，「只要我们有一口吃的，就短不了您的。我们到最后一块干粮也分您一半。」
「谢谢您，莉薇太太，」简动情地答道，「俺知道谁是俺的朋友，俺什么都不愁了。可是看在主的份上，菲利普老爷，快把孩子递给俺，让俺拍拍他的背，不然他要把自己憋死了！」
头一个看出小多迪不知为什么喘不上气来的，正是老保姆。她一把从少校怀里接过孩子，在他背上拍着，轻轻地颠着。这么治了一阵，孩子不倒气了，可还喘得粗，带着怪怪的啸声。
「哎呀，我的孩子！」母亲大惊失色，把孩子接到自己怀里，「他这是怎么了，简奶奶？」
「老天爷，太太，俺不知道，别是吞了什么；他手里除了波莉太太给的那个摇铃，什么也没拿过呀。」
卡特里特太太一把抓起那只象牙摇铃——它用一根缎带挂在婴儿颈上。
「他把柄头上旋的那个小圆头吞下去了，」她吓得脸发白，喊道，「卡在嗓子眼了。快打电话叫普赖斯医生立刻来，菲利普，别等我的宝宝憋死了！哦，我的宝宝，我的心肝！」
提心吊胆地过了半个钟头，孩子躺着还安静，只是喘得费劲。普赖斯医生到了，悬念才解；他把孩子细细查了一遍。
「真是桩少有的事故，」查完他宣布道，「就我查到的看，那块象牙吸进了气管，卡在右支气管口上。我试试不开刀把它弄出来，但保不齐成不成。」
又过了半个钟头，普赖斯医生宣布：不动大手术，那堵着的东西取不出来。

===Original===

“I do not see,” he declared, “how an operation can be avoided.”
“Will it be dangerous?” inquired the major anxiously, while Mrs. Carteret shivered at the thought.
“It will be necessary to cut into his throat from the outside. All such operations are more or less dangerous, especially on small children. If this were some other child, I might undertake the operation unassisted; but I know how you value this one, major, and I should prefer to share the responsibility with a specialist.”
“Is there one in town?” asked the major.
“No, but we can get one from out of town.”
“Send for the best one in the country,” said the major, “who can be got here in time. Spare no expense, Dr. Price. We value this child above any earthly thing.”
“The best is the safest,” replied Dr. Price. “I will send for Dr. Burns, of Philadelphia, the best surgeon in that line in America. If he can start at once, he can reach here in sixteen or eighteen hours, and the case can wait even longer, if inflammation does not set in.”
The message was dispatched forthwith. By rare good fortune the eminent specialist was able to start within an hour or two after the receipt of Dr. Price’s telegram. Meanwhile the baby remained restless and uneasy, the doctor spending most of his time by its side. Mrs. Carteret, who had never been quite strong since the child’s birth, was a prey to the most agonizing apprehensions.
Mammy Jane, while not presuming to question the opinion of Dr. Price, and not wishing to add to her mistress’s distress, was secretly oppressed by forebodings which she was unable to shake off. The child was born for bad luck. The mole under its ear, just at the point where the hangman’s knot would strike, had foreshadowed dire misfortune. She had already observed several little things which had rendered her vaguely anxious.
For instance, upon one occasion, on entering the room where the baby had been left alone, asleep in his crib, she had met a strange cat hurrying from the nursery, and, upon examining closely the pillow upon which the child lay, had found a depression which had undoubtedly been due to the weight of the cat’s body. The child was restless and uneasy, and Jane had ever since believed that the cat had been sucking little Dodie’s breath, with what might have been fatal results had she not appeared just in the nick of time.

===Chinese===

「依我看，」他说，「手术是免不了的。」
「危险么？」少校焦急地问；卡特里特太太一想到手术就发抖。
「得从喉部外头切进去。这类手术都免不了几分危险，尤其是这么小的孩子。若是别人家的孩子，我也许就自己主刀了；可我知道这孩子在您二位心里多贵重，少校，我情愿同一位专家分担责任。」
「城里有一位么？」少校问。
「没有；不过外地请得来。」
「去请全国最好的、赶得来的，」少校说，「不惜工本，普赖斯医生。这孩子在我们眼里贵过世上一切。」
「请最好的最稳妥，」普赖斯医生答道，「我去请费城的伯恩斯医生，这一行全美第一刀。他若能立刻动身，十六到十八个钟头赶到；只要不发炎，病情再等等也使得。」
电报随即发出。天从人愿，那位大名鼎鼎的专家收到普赖斯医生的电报，一两个钟头内便动身了。这一路孩子一直烦躁不安，医生大半时候守在旁边。卡特里特太太自生育以来身子就没大好过，如今更被最熬人的忧惧咬噬着。
简奶奶对普赖斯医生的断语不敢置疑，也不愿添女主人的愁苦，心里却暗暗压着一层挥不去的凶兆。这孩子生来命硬。耳朵底下那颗痣——正卡在刽子手的绳结落下的地方——早就预示了大凶。她已经瞧出好几桩小事，叫她隐隐地不踏实。
比如有一回，她走进那间只留着孩子在摇篮里睡觉的屋子，迎面撞见一只野猫慌慌张张逃出育婴室；再细看孩子枕着的枕头，上面一个凹印子，分明是猫身子压出来的。孩子烦躁不安；打那以后简就认定，那猫是在吸小多迪的气——若不是她来得正巧，后果不堪设想。

===Original===

This untimely accident of the rattle, a fatality for which no one could be held responsible, had confirmed the unlucky omen. Jane’s duties in the nursery did not permit her to visit her friend the conjure woman; but she did find time to go out in the back yard at dusk, and to dig up the charm which she had planted there. It had protected the child so far; but perhaps its potency had become exhausted. She picked up the bottle, shook it vigorously, and then laid it back, with the other side up. Refilling the hole, she made a cross over the top with the thumb of her left hand, and walked three times around it.
What this strange symbolism meant, or whence it derived its origin, Aunt Jane did not know. The cross was there, and the Trinity, though Jane was scarcely conscious of these, at this moment, as religious emblems. But she hoped, on general principles, that this performance would strengthen the charm and restore little Dodie’s luck. It certainly had its moral effect upon Jane’s own mind, for she was able to sleep better, and contrived to impress Mrs. Carteret with her own hopefulness.

===Chinese===

摇铃这桩飞来的横祸——一桩谁也怪不着的劫数——把这不祥的兆头坐实了。育婴室里的活计绊着简奶奶的脚，叫她无法去访她那位巫婆朋友；可黄昏时分，她倒真抽空到了后院，把先前埋在那里的护符刨了出来。它护佑这孩子到如今；许是法力使尽了。她拾起那只小瓶，使劲摇了摇，又放回去，换了一面朝上。把坑填平，她用左手大拇指在上面画了个十字，绕着它走了三圈。
这套古怪的仪轨是什么意思、从哪里传来，简婆婆不知道。十字在那里，三位一体也在那里——只是此刻的简，几乎不曾把它们当作宗教的标记。她只是照一般的道理指望：这一番做法能叫护符灵光复振，把小多迪的运道找回来。这做法在她自己心上确实收了安神的效验——她睡得安稳些了，还想法子叫卡特里特太太也沾了她的乐观。

## XXIV Two Southern Gentlemen / 两位南方绅士
===Original===

The iron bolt rattled in the lock, the door of a cell swung open, and when Mr. Delamere had entered was quickly closed again.
“Well, Sandy!”
“Oh, Mars John! Is you fell from hebben ter he’p me out er here? I prayed de Lawd ter sen’ you, an’ He answered my prayer, an’ here you is, Mars John﻿—here you is! Oh, Mars John, git me out er dis place!”
“Tut, tut, Sandy!” answered his master; “of course I’ll get you out. That’s what I’ve come for. How in the world did such a mistake ever happen? You would no more commit such a crime than I would!”
“No, suh, ’deed I wouldn’, an’ you know I wouldn’! I wouldn’ want ter bring no disgrace on de fam’ly dat raise’ me, ner ter make no trouble fer you, suh; but here I is, suh, lock’ up in jail, an’ folks talkin’ ’bout hangin’ me fer somethin’ dat never entered my min’, suh. I swea’ ter God I never thought er sech a thing!”
“Of course you didn’t, Sandy,” returned Mr. Delamere soothingly; “and now the next thing, and the simplest thing, is to get you out of this. I’ll speak to the officers, and at the preliminary hearing tomorrow I’ll tell them all about you, and they will let you go. You won’t mind spending one night in jail for your sins.”
“No, suh, ef I wuz sho’ I’d be ’lowed ter spen’ it here. But dey say dey ’re gwine ter lynch me ter-night﻿—I kin hear ’em talkin’ f’m de winders er de cell, suh.”
“Well, *I* say, Sandy, that they shall do no such thing! Lynch a man brought up by a Delamere, for a crime of which he is innocent? Preposterous! I’ll speak to the authorities and see that you are properly protected until this mystery is unraveled. If Tom had been here, he would have had you out before now, Sandy. My grandson is a genuine Delamere, is he not, Sandy?”
“Yas, suh, yas, suh,” returned Sandy, with a lack of enthusiasm which he tried to conceal from his master. “An’ I s’pose ef he hadn’ gone fishin’ so soon dis mawnin’, he’d ’a’ be’n lookin’ after me, suh.”
“It has been my love for him and your care of me, Sandy,” said the old gentleman tremulously, “that have kept me alive so long; but now explain to me everything concerning this distressing matter, and I shall then be able to state your case to better advantage.”
“Well, suh,” returned Sandy, “I mought’s well tell de whole tale an’ not hol’ nothin’ back.

===Chinese===

铁插销在锁里哗啦一响，牢房的门开了；德拉梅尔先生走进去，门随即又关上了。
「哎呀，桑迪！」
「哦，约翰老爷！您是从天上下来救俺出去的么？俺求老天爷把您派来，他应了俺的祷告，您这就来了，约翰老爷——您来了！哦，约翰老爷，快把俺弄出这地方吧！」
「嗐，嗐，桑迪！」主人答道，「自然要救你出去，我就是为这个来的。这误会究竟是怎么闹出来的？你绝不会犯这样的罪，正如我不会！」
「不会，老爷，实在不会，您知道俺不会！俺把俺带大的这家人家看得比什么都重，不能给它丢脸，也不能给您惹祸；可是俺如今蹲在这儿，老爷，关在牢里，人家说要为一件俺脑子里从来没有过的事吊死俺。俺对着上帝起誓，俺连想都没想过这样的事！」
「当然没有，桑迪，」德拉梅尔先生宽慰道，「眼下的事，也是最省事的事，就是把你弄出去。我这就去同管事的讲；明天预审，我把你的事原原本本一说，他们就会放你。为着你的罪过蹲一夜牢，你不嫌吧。」
「不嫌，老爷，只要准俺在这儿蹲这一夜。可他们说要今儿夜里私刑俺——俺在这牢房的窗户里听得见他们说话，老爷。」
「可俺偏说，桑迪，他们休想！私刑一个德拉梅尔家带大的人，为一桩他清白无辜的罪？荒唐透顶！我去同官府说，叫他们好好保护你，等这谜底揭开。汤姆要是在家，早就把你弄出去了，桑迪。我的孙子是个十足的德拉梅尔，是不是，桑迪？」
「是，老爷，是，」桑迪答道，声音里那点不起劲，他还在老爷跟前遮掩。「俺想他今儿早晨要不是急着去钓鱼，也该来看顾俺了，老爷。」
「正是我对他的爱、你对我的照看，桑迪，」老先生颤着声说，「撑着我活到今天。如今你把这件伤心事的来龙去脉，一五一十都讲给我听，我好把你的案情说得更有利些。」
「那么，老爷，」桑迪答道，「俺索性从头全说了，一个字也不瞒——

===Original===

I wuz kind er lonesome las’ night, an’ sence I be’n tu’ned outen de chu’ch on account er dat cakewalk I didn’ go ter, so he’p me God! I didn’ feel like gwine ter prayer-meetin’, so I went roun’ ter see Solomon Williams, an’ he wa’n’t home, an’ den I walk’ down street an’ met Josh Green, an’ he ax’ me inter Sam Taylor’s place, an’ I sot roun’ dere wid Josh till ’bout ’leven o’clock, w’en I sta’ted back home. I went straight ter de house, suh, an’ went ter bed an’ ter sleep widout sayin’ a wo’d ter a single soul excep’ Mistuh Tom, who wuz settin’ up readin’ a book w’en I come in. I wish I may drap dead in my tracks, suh, ef dat ain’t de God’s truf, suh, eve’y wo’d of it!”
“I believe every word of it, Sandy; now tell me about the clothes that you are said to have been found cleaning, and the suspicious articles that were found in your room?”
“Dat’s w’at beats me, Mars John,” replied Sandy, shaking his head mournfully. “Wen I lef home las’ night after supper, my clo’s wuz all put erway in de closet in my room, folded up on de she’f ter keep de moths out. Dey wuz my good clo’s﻿—de blue coat dat you wo’ ter de weddin’ fo’ty years ago, an’ dem dere plaid pants I gun Mistuh Cohen fo’ dollars fer three years ago; an’ w’en I looked in my closet dis mawnin’, suh, befo’ I got ready ter sta’t fer Belleview, dere wuz my clo’s layin’ on de flo’, all muddy an’ crumple’ up, des lack somebody had wo’ ’em in a fight! Somebody e’se had wo’ my clo’s﻿—er e’se dere’d be’n some witchcraf, er some sort er devilment gwine on dat I can’t make out, suh, ter save my soul!”
“There was no witchcraft, Sandy, but that there was some deviltry might well be. Now, what other negro, who might have been mistaken for you, could have taken your clothes? Surely no one about the house?”
“No, suh, no, suh. It couldn’t ’a’ be’n Jeff, fer he wuz at Belleview wid you; an’ it couldn’t ’a’ be’n Billy, fer he wuz too little ter wear my clo’s; an’ it couldn’t ’a’ be’n Sally, fer she’s a ’oman. It’s a myst’ry ter me, suh!”
“Have you no enemies? Is there anyone in Wellington whom you imagine would like to do you an injury?”
“Not a livin’ soul dat I knows of, suh. I’ve be’n tu’ned out’n de chu’ch, but I don’ know who my enemy is dere, er ef it wuz all a mistake, like dis yer jailin’ is; but de Debbil is in dis somewhar, Mars John﻿—an’ I got my reasons fer sayin’ so.”
“What do you mean, Sandy?”

===Chinese===

「——昨儿夜里俺有点闷得慌；自打为那场俺没去成的步态舞叫教会撵出来——上帝保佑俺！——俺就不大想上祷告会，便绕到所罗门·威廉斯家去看他，他又不在家；俺就顺街往下走，碰见乔希·格林，他拉俺进了萨姆·泰勒的铺子，俺同乔希在那儿坐着，坐到十一点来钟才动身回家。俺一径到家，老爷，上床就睡着了，没同一个人说过一句话，只除了汤姆先生——俺进门时他正坐着看书。俺要是有一句瞎话，叫俺当场一头栽死，老爷，句句是老天爷的真理！」
「我一个字也信，桑迪。再说说你那身衣裳——人家说搜出来时你正在刷洗它——还有你屋里搜出的那些可疑东西，是怎么回事？」
「这正是把俺难住的地方，约翰老爷，」桑迪答道，凄凄惶惶地摇头。「昨儿傍晚俺出门的时候，俺的衣裳都收在俺屋的柜子里，叠得方方正正摤在架上，怕虫子咬。那是俺的好衣裳——四十年前您穿去吃喜酒的那件蓝外套，还有三年前俺花科恩先生四块钱买的那条格子裤；今儿早晨俺收拾动身去贝尔维尤之前，往柜子里一瞧，老爷，俺的衣裳撂在地上，全是泥，皱成一团，活像叫人穿着打过一架！是别人穿了俺的衣裳——再不就是有巫术，有什么俺说不上来的邪魔外道在里面作怪，老爷，俺拿性命担保也猜不透！」
「没有什么巫术，桑迪；说有人使坏，倒是不错的。那么，哪个别的黑人可能叫人错认成你，拿得走你的衣裳？总不会是家里的什么人吧？」
「不会，老爷，不会。不会是杰夫，他跟您在贝尔维尤；不会是比利，他个子太小，穿不得俺的衣裳；也不会是萨莉，她是女人。俺真猜不透，老爷！」
「你没有仇人么？惠灵顿有谁，你想来会存心害你？」
「俺知道的一个也没有，老爷。俺是叫教会撵出来了，可那边的仇人是谁俺不知道，兴许也同这场坐牢一样，全是一场误会；可是魔鬼掺在里头，约翰老爷——俺这么说是有来历的。」
「你指什么，桑迪？」

===Original===

Sandy related his experience of the preceding evening: how he had seen the apparition preceding him to the house, and how he had questioned Tom upon the subject.
“There’s some mystery here, Sandy,” said Mr. Delamere reflectively. “Have you told me all, now, upon your honor? I am trying to save your life, Sandy, and I must be able to trust your word implicitly. You must tell me every circumstance; a very little and seemingly unimportant bit of evidence may sometimes determine the issue of a great lawsuit. There is one thing especially, Sandy: where did you get the gold which was found in your trunk?”
Sandy’s face lit up with hopefulness.
“Why, Mars John, I kin ’splain dat part easy. Dat wuz money I had lent out, an’ I got back f’m﻿—But no, suh, I promise’ not ter tell.”
“Circumstances absolve you from your promise, Sandy. Your life is of more value to you than any other thing. If you will explain where you got the gold, and the silk purse that contained it, which is said to be Mrs. Ochiltree’s, you will be back home before night.”
Old Mr. Delamere’s faculties, which had been waning somewhat in sympathy with his health, were stirred to unusual acuteness by his servant’s danger. He was watching Sandy with all the awakened instincts of the trial lawyer. He could see clearly enough that, in beginning to account for the possession of the gold, Sandy had started off with his explanation in all sincerity. At the mention of the silk purse, however, his face had blanched to an ashen gray, and the words had frozen upon his lips.
A less discerning observer might have taken these things as signs of guilt, but not so Mr. Delamere.
“Well, Sandy,” said his master encouragingly, “go on. You got the gold from”﻿—
Sandy remained silent. He had had a great shock, and had taken a great resolution.
“Mars John,” he asked dreamily, “you don’ b’lieve dat I done dis thing?”
“Certainly not, Sandy, else why should I be here?”
“An’ nothin’ wouldn’ make you b’lieve it, suh?”
“No, Sandy﻿—I could not believe it of you. I’ve known you too long and too well.”
“An’ you wouldn’ b’lieve it, not even ef I wouldn’ say one wo’d mo’ about it?”
“No, Sandy, I believe you no more capable of this crime than I would be﻿—or my grandson, Tom.

===Chinese===

桑迪便把前一夜的遭遇讲了一遍：他怎样看见那身影在他头里往宅子去，又怎样为这事问过汤姆。
「这里头有个谜，桑迪，」德拉梅尔先生沉吟道。「都对我说了么？凭你的名誉。我在救你的命，桑迪，你的话我必须能一字不疑地信。什么情节都得告诉我；一件顶小、看着顶不要紧的证据，有时就能定一场大官司的输赢。特别有一件，桑迪：你箱子里搜出的金子，是哪里来的？」
桑迪的脸亮起了指望。
「嗐，约翰老爷，这一节俺一说就明白。那是俺借出去的钱，俺收回来——从——不成了，老爷，俺许过人不说的。」
「事到如今，你的许诺可以不算了，桑迪。你的性命比什么都贵重。你只要说明金子是哪里来的，还有装金子的那只绸钱包——人家说是奥奇尔特里太太的——天黑以前你就能到家。」
老德拉梅尔先生的官能，本来随着身子一同衰败，此刻叫仆人的危难激得异常敏锐。他用出庭律师骤然醒转的全部本能，端详着桑迪。他看得清清楚楚：桑迪起头讲那金子的来历，本是句句真心；可一提到那只绸钱包，他的脸刷地灰白，话在嘴边冻住了。
眼力差些的人也许会把这些当作罪证的记号，德拉梅尔先生却不。
「说下去呀，桑迪，」主人鼓励道，「那金子，你是从——」
桑迪不作声。他受了一个大大的震动，也下了一个大大的决心。
「约翰老爷，」他恍恍惚惚地问，「您不信这事是俺干的？」
「自然不信，桑迪；不然我到这里来做什么？」
「什么也拦不住您信么，老爷？」
「拦不住，桑迪——我断不会信到你头上。我同你相识得太久、太深了。」
「俺哪怕再不说一个字，您也不信？」
「不信，桑迪。论这桩罪，俺相信你同我一样干不出来——同我孙子汤姆一样。」

===Original===

I wish Tom were here, that he might help me overcome your stubbornness; but you’ll not be so foolish, so absurdly foolish, Sandy, as to keep silent and risk your life merely to shield someone else, when by speaking you might clear up this mystery and be restored at once to liberty. Just tell me where you got the gold,” added the old gentleman persuasively. “Come, now, Sandy, that’s a good fellow!”
“Mars John,” asked Sandy softly, “w’en my daddy, ’way back yander befo’ de wah, wuz about ter be sol’ away f’m his wife an’ child’en, you bought him an’ dem, an’ kep’ us all on yo’ place tergether, didn’t you, suh?”
“Yes, Sandy, and he was a faithful servant, and proved worthy of all I did for him.”
“And w’en he had wo’ked fer you ten years, suh, you sot ’im free?”
“Yes, Sandy, he had earned his freedom.”
“An’ w’en de wah broke out, an’ my folks wuz scattered, an’ I didn’ have nothin’ ter do ner nowhar ter go, you kep’ me on yo’ place, and tuck me ter wait on you, suh, didn’t you?”
“Yes, Sandy, and you have been a good servant and a good friend; but tell me now about this gold, and I’ll go and get you out of this, right away, for I need you, Sandy, and you’ll not be of any use to me shut up here!”
“Jes’ hol’ on a minute befo’ you go, Mars John; fer ef dem people outside should git holt er me befo’ you *does* git me out er here, I may never see you no mo’, suh, in dis worl’. W’en Mars Billy McLean shot me by mistake, w’ile we wuz out huntin’ dat day, who wuz it boun’ up my woun’s an’ kep’ me from bleedin’ ter def, an’ kyar’ed me two miles on his own shoulders ter a doctuh?”
“Yes, Sandy, and when black Sally ran away with your young mistress and Tom, when Tom was a baby, who stopped the runaway, and saved their lives at the risk of his own?”
“Dat wa’n’t nothin’, suh; anybody could ’a’ done dat, w’at wuz strong ernuff an’ swif’ ernuff. You is be’n good ter me, suh, all dese years, an’ I’ve tried ter do my duty by you, suh, an’ by Mistuh Tom, who wuz yo’ own gran’son, an’ de las’ one er de fam’ly.”
“Yes, you have, Sandy, and when I am gone, which will not be very long, Tom will take care of you, and see that you never want. But we are wasting valuable time, Sandy, in these old reminiscences. Let us get back to the present. Tell me about the gold, now, so that I may at once look after your safety. It may not even be necessary for you to remain here all night.”

===Chinese===

「汤姆要是在这儿，正好叫他帮我磨磨你这股犟劲；可你不至于糊涂到这步田地，不至于糊涂得这样离谱，桑迪——只为遮掩别人，宁可闭紧嘴巴拿性命去押；其实你一开口，这谜就破了，你当场就能出去。只消告诉我那金子哪里来的，」老先生劝道，「来，桑迪，这才是好样的！」
「约翰老爷，」桑迪轻声问，「早年间，打仗以前，俺爹眼看要从俺娘和孩子们身边卖走了，是您把他连他们娘儿几个一并买了下来，把俺们一家子拢在一处养在您庄园上，是不是，老爷？」
「是的，桑迪；他是个忠心的仆人，我为他办的那些事，他都受之无愧。」
「他给您干了十年活，老爷，您放他自由了？」
「是的，桑迪，他的自由是他挣下的。」
「后来开了仗，俺家里人散了，俺没事做、没处去，您把俺留在庄园上，带在身边伺候您，老爷，是不是？」
「是的，桑迪；你也一直是好仆人、好朋友。如今快说说这金子，我这就去把你弄出去——我用得着你，桑迪；你蹲在这里，于我一点用处没有！」
「您去之前再听俺说一句，约翰老爷；外头那些人要是当真在您把俺弄出去之前抢了先，俺兴许就再见不着您了，老爷，在这个世上。那年比利·麦克莱恩少爷错把俺打了枪、咱们在外头打猎那天，是谁给俺裹的伤、止住血没叫俺流死，又把俺背在自家肩膀上驱了两里地找医生？」
「是的，桑迪；还有黑萨莉抱着你少奶奶和小汤姆跑掉那次——汤姆那时还是娃娃——是谁截住那惊马，豁出自己的命救下他们娘儿俩？」
「那不算什么，老爷；身子骨结实、腿脚快的人谁都做得到。这些年您待俺好，老爷，俺也尽心尽着俺的本分，对您，也对汤姆先生——他是您的亲孙子，这一门最后的骨血。」
「正是，桑迪。我去了以后——那也不会久了——汤姆会照应你，叫你永远不缺什么。可咱们在这些老话上白费工夫了，桑迪。说眼前的。快讲那金子，我好马上去保你的平安。兴许连这一夜都用不着你在这里过。」

===Original===

“Jes’ one wo’d mo’, Mars John, befo’ you go! I know you’re gwine ter do de bes’ you kin fer me, an’ I’m sorry I can’t he’p you no mo’ wid it; but ef dere should be any accident, er ef you *can’t* git me out er here, don’ bother yo’ min’ ’bout it no mo’, suh, an’ don’ git yo’se’f ixcited, fer you know de doctuh says, suh, dat you can’t stan’ ixcitement; but jes’ leave me in de han’s er de Lawd, suh﻿—*He’ll* look after me, here er hereafter. I know I’ve fell f’m grace mo’ d’n once, but I’ve done made my peace wid Him in dis here jailhouse, suh, an’ I ain’t ’feared ter die﻿—ef I haf ter. I ain’ got no wife ner child’n ter mo’n fer me, an’ I’ll die knowin’ dat I’ve done my duty ter dem dat hi’ed me, an’ trusted me, an’ had claims on me. Fer I wuz raise’ by a Delamere, suh, an’ all de ole Delameres wuz gent’emen, an’ deir principles spread ter de niggers ’round ’em, suh; an’ ef I has ter die fer somethin’ I didn’ do﻿—I kin die, suh, like a gent’eman! But ez fer dat gol’, suh, I ain’ gwine ter say one wo’d mo’ ’bout it ter nobody in dis worl’!”
Nothing could shake Sandy’s determination. Mr. Delamere argued, expostulated, but all in vain. Sandy would not speak.
More and more confident of some mystery, which would come out in time, if properly investigated, Mr. Delamere, strangely beset by a vague sense of discomfort over and beyond that occasioned by his servant’s danger, hurried away upon his errand of mercy. He felt less confident of the outcome than when he had entered the jail, but was quite as much resolved that no effort should be spared to secure protection for Sandy until there had been full opportunity for the truth to become known.
“Take good care of your prisoner, sheriff,” he said sternly, as he was conducted to the door. “He will not be long in your custody, and I shall see that you are held strictly accountable for his safety.”
“I’ll do what I can, sir,” replied the sheriff in an even tone and seemingly not greatly impressed by this warning. “If the prisoner is taken from me, it will be because the force that comes for him is too strong for resistance.”
“There should be no force too strong for an honest man in your position to resist﻿—whether successfully or not is beyond the question. The officer who is intimidated by threats, or by his own fears, is recreant to his duty, and no better than the mob which threatens him.

===Chinese===

「俺再求您一句，约翰老爷，您就走！俺知道您会为俺尽十分的力，俺对不住，帮不上您什么了；可万一出了什么闪失，万一您真把俺弄不出去，也千万别再为这个操心，老爷，也千万别动气——您知道医生说的，老爷，您禁不得动气；只管把俺交给老天爷——他自会看顾俺，在这儿也好，在那一世也好。俺知道俺失足堕过不止一回落恩典，可俺在这牢里已经同他和好了，老爷，俺不怕死——真到那一步的话。俺没有老婆孩子哭俺，俺死也死得明白：俺对雇俺、信俺、于俺有恩的人，尽到了本分。因为俺是德拉梅尔家带大的，老爷，老一辈的德拉梅尔个个是绅士，他们的道理也传到了身边黑人身上，老爷；俺要是为一件俺没干的事去死——俺也能死得像个绅士！至于那金子，老爷，这世上无论对谁，俺再不多说一个字！」
什么也摇不动桑迪的决心。德拉梅尔先生劝了又劝，责了又责，全是白费。桑迪就是不说。
德拉梅尔先生愈想愈认定这里头有一个谜，只要查问得法，迟早会水落石出；同时又有一种说不清的不自在，缠在他心头，压过仆人危难本身给他的——他怀着这桩慈善的差事，匆匆去了。比起进监狱时，他对结局少了几分把握；可是要保桑迪周全、要给真相留出充分的时机，这份不肯省一分力气的决心，一分未减。
「把你的囚犯好生看住，警长，」被引到门口时，他正色说，「他押在你手上不会久了；他若有三长两短，我定叫你严格负责。」
「我尽力吧，先生，」警长答得平平的，这话似乎没怎么打动他。「囚犯若真从我手里叫人夺了去，那也只能怪来的人太强，抵抗不动。」
「对一个身处你这个位置的诚实人，天下不该有一股强得抵抗不动的势力——成得了功倒在其次。叫威胁吓倒、叫自己的怕吓倒的官吏，便亏负了他的职守，同威胁他的暴民是一路货。」

===Original===

But you will have no such test, Mr. Wemyss! I shall see to it myself that there is no violence!”

===Chinese===

「可你受不着那样的考验，韦米斯先生！不许动粗的事，我亲自来管！」
